## Appendix D: Complete Code Implementation Examples

### D.1 LLM Assimilation Pipeline Implementation

```python
class AHpasAssimilationPipeline:
    """
    Phase 1: Artifact Assimilation
    Converts messy human artifacts into structured state for Jev
    """
    
    def __init__(self, llm_client, domain_config):
        self.llm = llm_client
        self.domain = domain_config
    
    def assimilate_artifacts(self, artifacts: Dict[str, Any]) -> StructuredState:
        """
        Main entry point: converts raw human artifacts to Jev-ready state
        
        artifacts example (construction domain):
        {
            "superintendent_notebooks": [file1.pdf, file2.pdf, ...],
            "rfi_exchanges": [rfi1.json, rfi2.json, ...],
            "subcontractor_communications": [comm1.pdf, ...],
            "safety_observation_cards": [card1.jpg, ...],
            "project_outcomes": {project_id: {cost_variance: 0.12, ...}}
        }
        """
        
        # Step 1: Extract raw text and structure from each artifact type
        extracted = self._extract_content(artifacts)
        
        # Step 2: Identify human patterns using domain-specific prompts
        patterns = self._identify_patterns(extracted)
        
        # Step 3: Build structured state for Jev consumption
        state = self._build_jev_state(patterns, artifacts)
        
        # Step 4: Validate state completeness
        self._validate_state(state)
        
        return state
    
    def _extract_content(self, artifacts):
        """Extract text, metadata, and relationships from artifacts"""
        
        extracted = {
            "text_content": [],
            "temporal_markers": [],
            "people_involved": [],
            "decisions_made": [],
            "communication_patterns": []
        }
        
        for artifact_type, artifact_list in artifacts.items():
            for artifact in artifact_list:
                # Use LLM to extract structure from unstructured content
                structure = self.llm.generate(
                    output_schema=ArtifactStructure,
                    prompt=f"""
                    Extract structured information from this {artifact_type}:
                    {artifact.content}
                    
                    Identify:
                    1. Key decisions and who made them
                    2. Communication patterns (who talks to whom, about what)
                    3. Temporal information (when things happened)
                    4. Stated assumptions and implicit assumptions
                    5. Emotional indicators (stress, confidence, uncertainty)
                    6. Relationship dynamics (hierarchy, trust, conflict)
                    """
                )
                extracted["text_content"].append(structure)
        
        return extracted
    
    def _identify_patterns(self, extracted):
        """Identify human patterns using domain-specific pattern library"""
        
        # Load domain-specific patterns (from Appendix A)
        pattern_library = self.domain.pattern_library
        
        identified_patterns = []
        
        for pattern_name, pattern_def in pattern_library.items():
            # Use LLM to identify instances of this pattern
            instances = self.llm.analyze(
                prompt=f"""
                Search these extracted artifacts for instances of the 
                "{pattern_name}" pattern.
                
                Pattern definition: {pattern_def['description']}
                Indicators: {pattern_def['indicators']}
                Typical statistics: {pattern_def['statistics']}
                
                Extracted content: {extracted}
                
                Identify:
                1. Specific instances where this pattern occurred
                2. Severity or magnitude of each instance
                3. People involved
                4. Temporal context
                5. Outcome (if known)
                """,
                output_schema=PatternInstances
            )
            
            if instances.found:
                identified_patterns.append({
                    "pattern": pattern_name,
                    "instances": instances,
                    "statistical_context": pattern_def["statistics"]
                })
        
        return identified_patterns
    
    def _build_jev_state(self, patterns, artifacts):
        """Build structured state object for Jev judgment"""
        
        state = {
            "domain": self.domain.name,
            "patterns_identified": patterns,
            "artifacts_analyzed": len(artifacts),
            "people_involved": self._extract_people(artifacts),
            "temporal_scope": self._extract_timeframe(artifacts),
            "outcome_data": artifacts.get("outcomes", {}),
            
            # Domain-specific state fields
            "human_process_data": artifacts.get("human_process_data", {}),
            "tribal_knowledge_inventory": self._inventory_tribal_knowledge(patterns),
            "communication_flow_map": self._map_communications(patterns),
            "hierarchy_structure": self._map_hierarchy(patterns)
        }
        
        return state
```

### D.2 Question Battery Generation with Validation

```python
class QuestionBatteryGenerator:
    """
    Phase 2: Generate and validate adversarial question batteries
    """
    
    def __init__(self, llm_client, jev_client, domain_config):
        self.llm = llm_client
        self.jev = jev_client
        self.domain = domain_config
    
    def generate_battery(self, state: StructuredState, 
                         analysis_goals: List[str]) -> QuestionBattery:
        """
        Generate complete question battery for a specific analysis
        
        analysis_goals example:
        [
            "Identify decision quality issues",
            "Find communication breakdowns",
            "Assess knowledge transfer effectiveness",
            "Evaluate team dynamics impact"
        ]
        """
        
        # Step 1: Generate raw questions using LLM
        raw_questions = self._generate_raw_questions(state, analysis_goals)
        
        # Step 2: Apply three-tier validation
        validated = self._three_tier_validation(raw_questions)
        
        # Step 3: Pin battery version
        battery = self._pin_battery(validated)
        
        return battery
    
    def _generate_raw_questions(self, state, goals):
        """Generate questions using domain-specific templates"""
        
        questions = []
        
        for goal in goals:
            # Use domain-specific question templates
            templates = self.domain.question_templates[goal]
            
            for template in templates:
                generated = self.llm.generate(
                    output_schema=JevQuestion,
                    prompt=f"""
                    Generate adversarial questions for {goal} analysis.
                    
                    Domain: {self.domain.name}
                    State context: {state.summary}
                    
                    Template guidance:
                    - Primitive type: {template['primitive']}
                    - Question pattern: {template['pattern']}
                    - Criteria example: {template['criteria_example']}
                    
                    HARD RULES:
                    1. ONE narrow, coherent judgment per question
                    2. Complete meaning in instructions (IDs never reach model)
                    3. Criteria must describe concrete, self-contained situations
                    4. Use backticked paths for state references
                    5. Include no-match outcome where nothing fits
                    6. For Choice: verify candidate coverage
                    
                    Generate 5-10 questions following this template.
                    """
                )
                questions.extend(generated.questions)
        
        return questions
    
    def _three_tier_validation(self, questions):
        """
        Apply three-tier validation:
        Tier 1: Code validation (deterministic)
        Tier 2: Jev meta-judgment (calibrated)
        Tier 3: Human review (optional, for battery pinning)
        """
        
        # TIER 1: Code validation
        code_validated = []
        for q in questions:
            if self._validate_schema(q):
                if self._validate_atomicity(q):
                    if self._validate_state_references(q):
                        if self._validate_criteria(q):
                            code_validated.append(q)
        
        # TIER 2: Jev meta-judgment on question quality
        jev_validated = []
        for q in code_validated:
            quality_check = self.jev.ask(
                state={"draft_question": q},
                questions=[
                    noul("Does `draft_question.instructions` ask two separate "
                         "things that could be answered differently?"),
                    noul("Could `draft_question.criteria` be understood "
                         "without reading any other text?"),
                    noul("Does `draft_question` reference information by "
                         "position or ID rather than including it?"),
                    noul("Is `draft_question` actually adversarial or just "
                         "confirmatory?")
                ]
            )
            
            # If any quality check fails, send back for repair
            if not any(quality_check.probabilities[p] > 0.6 
                      for p in ["two_things", "unclear_criteria", 
                               "position_reference", "not_adversarial"]):
                jev_validated.append(q)
            else:
                # Send back to LLM for repair
                repaired = self._repair_question(q, quality_check)
                jev_validated.extend(repaired)
        
        return jev_validated
    
    def _validate_schema(self, question):
        """Tier 1a: Schema compliance"""
        required_fields = ["instructions", "primitive", "state_refs"]
        if question.primitive in ["choice", "score"]:
            required_fields.append("criteria" if question.primitive == "score" 
                                   else "options")
        
        return all(hasattr(question, field) for field in required_fields)
    
    def _validate_atomicity(self, question):
        """Tier 1b: One judgment per question"""
        # Check for conjunctions that might indicate compound questions
        compound_indicators = [" and ", " but also ", " as well as ", 
                              " in addition to "]
        return not any(indicator in question.instructions.lower() 
                      for indicator in compound_indicators)
    
    def _validate_state_references(self, question):
        """Tier 1c: State reference validity"""
        # Check that backticked paths exist in state
        import re
        paths = re.findall(r'`([^`]+)`', question.instructions)
        # In real implementation, would check against actual state object
        return len(paths) > 0 or "state_refs" in question
    
    def _validate_criteria(self, question):
        """Tier 1d: Criteria completeness"""
        if question.primitive == "score":
            return (hasattr(question, "criteria") and 
                    len(question.criteria) >= 3 and
                    all(isinstance(c, str) and len(c) > 10 
                        for c in question.criteria))
        return True
```

### D.3 Jev Execution Engine with Escalation

```python
class JevExecutionEngine:
    """
    Phase 3: Execute judgments at scale with escalation cascade
    """
    
    def __init__(self, jev_client, escalation_config):
        self.jev = jev_client
        self.escalation = escalation_config
    
    def execute_battery(self, battery: QuestionBattery, 
                       state: StructuredState) -> JudgmentResults:
        """
        Execute all questions in battery against state
        
        Returns: JudgmentResults with typed answers, probabilities, 
                 and confidence scores
        """
        
        results = {
            "total_judgments": 0,
            "high_confidence_findings": [],
            "low_confidence_flags": [],
            "escalation_needed": [],
            "summary_statistics": {}
        }
        
        # Execute all questions in parallel (they're independent)
        all_answers = self.jev.ask(
            state=state,
            questions=[q.to_jev_format() for q in battery.questions]
        )
        
        # Process results
        for question, answer in zip(battery.questions, all_answers):
            results["total_judgments"] += 1
            
            # Check for high-confidence findings
            if self._is_high_confidence_finding(question, answer):
                results["high_confidence_findings"].append({
                    "question": question,
                    "answer": answer,
                    "significance": self._assess_significance(question, answer)
                })
            
            # Check for low-confidence flags (need escalation)
            elif self._needs_escalation(question, answer):
                results["low_confidence_flags"].append({
                    "question": question,
                    "answer": answer,
                    "escalation_reason": self._determine_escalation_reason(answer)
                })
        
        # Calculate summary statistics
        results["summary_statistics"] = self._calculate_statistics(results)
        
        return results
    
    def _is_high_confidence_finding(self, question, answer):
        """Determine if this is a high-confidence finding"""
        
        # For Noul: high probability of problem
        if question.primitive == "noul":
            return answer.probability > 0.7
        
        # For Score: extreme scores with high confidence
        elif question.primitive == "score":
            return (answer.confidence > 0.7 and 
                    (answer.score <= 1 or answer.score >= 4))
        
        # For Choice: clear winner with high confidence
        elif question.primitive == "choice":
            return (answer.confidence > 0.7 and 
                    max(answer.probabilities.values()) > 0.6)
        
        return False
    
    def _needs_escalation(self, question, answer):
        """Determine if this needs escalation to pi-autoresearch or LLM"""
        
        # Low confidence on important question
        if answer.confidence < 0.4:
            return True
        
        # Ambiguous result (near 0.5 for Noul)
        if question.primitive == "noul" and 0.4 < answer.probability < 0.6:
            return True
        
        # Spread distribution on Choice (competing hypotheses)
        if question.primitive == "choice":
            probs = list(answer.probabilities.values())
            if max(probs) - min(probs) < 0.2:  # Nearly uniform
                return True
        
        return False
    
    def run_escalation_cascade(self, results: JudgmentResults,
                              state: StructuredState) -> EscalatedResults:
        """
        Run escalation cascade for flagged findings
        
        Level 1: Already done (Jev triage)
        Level 2: pi-autoresearch investigation
        Level 3: LLM deep analysis
        Level 4: Human expert review (outside this system)
        """
        
        escalated = {
            "pi_research_findings": [],
            "llm_deep_analyses": [],
            "human_review_items": []
        }
        
        # LEVEL 2: pi-autoresearch investigation
        for flag in results["low_confidence_flags"]:
            investigation = self._pi_research_investigate(flag, state)
            escalated["pi_research_findings"].append(investigation)
        
        # LEVEL 3: LLM deep analysis for complex findings
        for finding in results["high_confidence_findings"]:
            if finding["significance"] > 0.8:  # Very significant
                deep_analysis = self._llm_deep_analyze(finding, state)
                escalated["llm_deep_analyses"].append(deep_analysis)
        
        # LEVEL 4: Identify items needing human review
        escalated["human_review_items"] = self._identify_human_review_items(
            escalated
        )
        
        return escalated
```

### D.4 pi-autoresearch Integration

```python
class PiAutoResearchIntegration:
    """
    Phase 4: Pattern discovery and hypothesis testing
    """
    
    def __init__(self, pi_research_client, domain_config):
        self.pi = pi_research_client
        self.domain = domain_config
    
    def discover_patterns(self, judgment_results: JudgmentResults,
                         state: StructuredState) -> DiscoveredPatterns:
        """
        Discover human workflow patterns that predict outcomes
        """
        
        # Design experiment based on judgment results
        experiment = self.pi.design_experiment(
            hypothesis=f"Specific human patterns in {self.domain.name} "
                      f"predict outcomes",
            
            variables=self._extract_variables_from_judgments(judgment_results),
            
            outcome_metrics=self._map_outcomes(state),
            
            data_source=state.historical_data_reference
        )
        
        # Execute pattern discovery
        results = self.pi.execute(experiment)
        
        # Validate discovered patterns
        validated = self._validate_patterns(results)
        
        return validated
    
    def _extract_variables_from_judgments(self, results):
        """Extract human factor variables from judgment results"""
        
        variables = {}
        
        # Extract from high-confidence findings
        for finding in results["high_confidence_findings"]:
            pattern_type = finding["question"].pattern_type
            
            if pattern_type == "team_dynamics":
                variables["team_stability"] = self._categorize_team_stability(
                    finding["answer"]
                )
            elif pattern_type == "communication":
                variables["communication_method"] = self._categorize_communication(
                    finding["answer"]
                )
            elif pattern_type == "hierarchy":
                variables["hierarchy_openness"] = self._categorize_hierarchy(
                    finding["answer"]
                )
            # ... other pattern types
        
        return variables
    
    def run_counterfactual_simulation(self, current_state: StructuredState,
                                     proposed_intervention: Dict) -> SimulationResult:
        """
        Simulate what would happen if intervention were applied
        """
        
        simulation = self.pi.design_experiment(
            hypothesis=f"If {proposed_intervention['description']} were "
                      f"implemented, outcomes would improve",
            
            variables={
                "intervention": proposed_intervention,
                "current_patterns": current_state.patterns,
                "historical_similar_cases": self._find_similar_interventions(
                    proposed_intervention
                )
            },
            
            outcome_metrics=[
                "probability_of_improvement",
                "magnitude_of_improvement",
                "time_to_improvement",
                "potential_unintended_consequences"
            ]
        )
        
        result = self.pi.execute(simulation)
        
        return SimulationResult(
            probability_of_success=result.improvement_probability,
            expected_magnitude=result.expected_improvement,
            confidence_interval=result.confidence_bounds,
            based_on_n_cases=result.sample_size,
            caveats=result.limitations
        )
```

### D.5 Planning Mode Implementation

```python
class PlanningModeAnalyzer:
    """
    Planning Mode: Analyze prospective plans against historical patterns
    """
    
    def __init__(self, llm_client, jev_client, pi_research_client,
                 similar_case_engine):
        self.llm = llm_client
        self.jev = jev_client
        self.pi = pi_research_client
        self.case_engine = similar_case_engine
    
    def analyze_plan(self, planning_artifacts: Dict[str, Any],
                    plan_type: str) -> PlanningAnalysis:
        """
        Main entry point for planning analysis
        
        plan_type examples: "business_launch", "non_profit_launch",
                           "film_production", "pe_investment", 
                           "novel_writing", "marketing_plan"
        """
        
        # Step 1: Ingest and structure planning artifacts
        structured_plan = self._structure_plan(planning_artifacts)
        
        # Step 2: Retrieve similar historical cases
        similar_cases = self.case_engine.find_similar(
            structured_plan, plan_type
        )
        
        # Step 3: Generate planning-specific question battery
        battery = self._generate_planning_battery(
            structured_plan, similar_cases, plan_type
        )
        
        # Step 4: Execute judgments
        judgment_results = self.jev.execute_battery(
            battery, 
            state={
                "plan": structured_plan,
                "similar_cases": similar_cases,
                "historical_outcomes": similar_cases.outcomes
            }
        )
        
        # Step 5: Run pi-autoresearch for pattern validation
        pattern_validation = self.pi.discover_patterns(
            judgment_results,
            state={"plan": structured_plan, "cases": similar_cases}
        )
        
        # Step 6: Generate recommendations
        recommendations = self.llm.synthesize(
            prompt=f"""
            Based on analysis of this {plan_type} against {len(similar_cases)} 
            similar historical cases:
            
            Judgment results: {judgment_results}
            Pattern validation: {pattern_validation}
            
            Generate specific, evidence-based recommendations:
            1. Critical risks identified (with probability estimates)
            2. Specific modifications that improved similar plans
            3. Team composition suggestions based on historical patterns
            4. Timeline/financial adjustments based on calibration
            5. What this plan does well (preserve these)
            
            Do NOT invent findings not present in judgments.
            """,
            output_format="planning_recommendations"
        )
        
        return PlanningAnalysis(
            structured_plan=structured_plan,
            similar_cases=similar_cases,
            judgment_results=judgment_results,
            pattern_validation=pattern_validation,
            recommendations=recommendations,
            overall_risk_score=self._calculate_risk_score(judgment_results),
            confidence_assessment=self._assess_confidence(judgment_results)
        )
    
    def _generate_planning_battery(self, plan, cases, plan_type):
        """Generate planning-specific questions"""
        
        templates = {
            "assumption_realism": {
                "primitive": "noul",
                "pattern": "Is this {assumption_type} realistic given "
                          "historical performance of similar {plan_type}?",
                "state_refs": ["plan.assumptions", "cases.outcomes"]
            },
            "completeness_detection": {
                "primitive": "choice",
                "pattern": "Which common failure category does this plan "
                          "NOT address?",
                "options": self._get_failure_categories(plan_type)
            },
            "team_prospective": {
                "primitive": "score",
                "pattern": "How well does this team composition match "
                          "requirements?",
                "criteria": ["Severe mismatch", "Several gaps", "Adequate",
                            "Well-matched", "Exceptionally suited"]
            },
            "blind_spot_detection": {
                "primitive": "noul",
                "pattern": "Does this plan exhibit the pattern that "
                          "preceded {failure_mode} in similar cases?"
            },
            "assumption_conflict": {
                "primitive": "noul",
                "pattern": "Do these assumptions contradict each other "
                          "or the strategy?"
            },
            "prediction_calibration": {
                "primitive": "score",
                "pattern": "How well calibrated is this creator's "
                          "prediction track record?",
                "criteria": ["Systematically overoptimistic", "Overoptimistic",
                            "Well-calibrated", "Conservative",
                            "Systematically conservative"]
            }
        }
        
        # Generate specific questions from templates
        questions = []
        for template_name, template in templates.items():
            generated = self.llm.generate_questions(
                template=template,
                plan=plan,
                cases=cases,
                plan_type=plan_type
            )
            questions.extend(generated)
        
        return QuestionBattery(questions=questions)
```

---

## Appendix E: Human Process Data Collection Guide

### E.1 When Human Process Data Doesn't Exist

```
SITUATION: Organization wants AHPAS analysis but has no Human Process Data

OPTION 1: RETROSPECTIVE RECONSTRUCTION
─────────────────────────────────────────
What: Go back through historical records to count patterns
Who: Interns, analysts, or researchers
Time: 4-12 weeks depending on volume
Example: Construction firm's insurance broker asked intern to classify 
         67 projects' weather delays as predictable/borderline/unpredictable
Result: Discovered 71% of "weather delays" were for predictable weather
Cost: Intern salary for 6 weeks

OPTION 2: PROSPECTIVE INSTRUMENTATION
─────────────────────────────────────────
What: Start counting things now for future analysis
Who: Current staff (add to existing workload)
Time: 3-6 months before enough data accumulates
Example: Hospital starts handoff observation study with research team
Result: 4,100 handoffs recorded over 14 months
Cost: Research team time

OPTION 3: ETHNOGRAPHIC OBSERVATION
─────────────────────────────────────────
What: Embed observers in workflow
Who: Trained observers or researchers
Time: 2-8 weeks intensive
Example: Observer follows superintendent for 2 weeks, records all 
         decisions and communications
Result: Rich qualitative data + some quantifiable patterns
Cost: Observer salary

OPTION 4: ADMINISTRATIVE LEVERAGING
─────────────────────────────────────────
What: Find existing counts nobody knew about
Who: Ask around, look in filing cabinets
Time: 1-2 weeks of archaeology
Example: VC firm discovers departing analyst's spreadsheet of all 
         investment votes and outcomes
Result: 10 years of voting data nobody knew existed
Cost: Discovery time only

RECOMMENDED SEQUENCE:
1. Try Option 4 first (cheapest, fastest)
2. If insufficient, Option 1 (retrospective)
3. If no historical records, Option 2 or 3
4. Always start Option 2 for future analyses
```

### E.2 Minimum Viable Human Process Data

```
FOR ANY AHPAS ANALYSIS, MINIMUM DATA NEEDED:

TIER 1: ESSENTIAL (without these, analysis impossible)
├── Outcome data (already exists in most organizations)
│   └── At minimum: success/failure binary for each case
├── People involved in each case
│   └── Who made decisions, who was on team
└── Temporal markers
    └── When things happened (even approximate)

TIER 2: STRONGLY DESIRED (dramatically improves analysis)
├── Experience levels of key people
│   └── Years in role, relevant background
├── Team composition history
│   └── Who worked with whom, for how long
└── Basic communication method
    └── Verbal/written/structured

TIER 3: OPTIMAL (enables pattern discovery)
├── Decision quality indicators
│   └── Proactive vs. reactive, anticipation vs. response
├── Communication effectiveness measures
│   └── Information retention, context preservation
├── Hierarchy openness indicators
│   └── Speaking-up culture, error reporting rates
└── Knowledge documentation status
    └── What's written vs. tribal
```

### E.3 Collection Instruments

```
INSTRUMENT 1: DAILY PREDICTION LOG (Construction/Engineering)
─────────────────────────────────────────
Format: Paper notebook, 3 prompts per day
Prompts:
1. "What do I think will happen tomorrow?"
2. "What am I worried about next week?"
3. "What would surprise me?"
Review: Weekly self-review of predictions vs. actuals
Data generated: Prediction accuracy, anticipation patterns
Time cost: 5 minutes/day

INSTRUMENT 2: HANDOFF OBSERVATION CHECKLIST (Medicine/Manufacturing)
─────────────────────────────────────────
Format: Structured observation form
Items tracked:
1. Items mentioned in handoff
2. Items remembered by receiver (asked 4 hours later)
3. Items needed for decisions (determined retrospectively)
4. Context vs. data transfer
Data generated: Retention rates by information type
Time cost: Observer time only

INSTRUMENT 3: DECISION RATIONALE JOURNAL (All domains)
─────────────────────────────────────────
Format: 2-sentence entry per significant decision
Prompts:
1. "Why this choice?"
2. "What alternatives were considered?"
Data generated: Decision quality patterns, assumption documentation
Time cost: 1 minute/decision

INSTRUMENT 4: NEAR-MISS HARVEST CARD (Safety-critical domains)
─────────────────────────────────────────
Format: Simple card, no-blame framing
Prompt: "What almost happened today?"
Data generated: Near-miss frequency (POSITIVE metric), pattern identification
Time cost: 2 minutes/incident

INSTRUMENT 5: TEAM CHEMISTRY PULSE (PM-Super, Partner-Associate, etc.)
─────────────────────────────────────────
Format: Monthly 1-10 rating by both parties
Prompt: "How effectively are we working together?"
Data generated: Relationship quality trajectory
Time cost: 1 minute/month
```

## Section 7 (Expanded): Original Analysis Dimensions

### 7.1 Complete Dimension Taxonomy

```
AHPAS ANALYZES ACROSS THESE DIMENSIONS:

A. SPECIFICATION & REQUIREMENTS DIMENSIONS
   A.1 Functional requirements completeness
   A.2 Non-functional requirements coverage
   A.3 Specification ambiguity detection
   A.4 Requirements traceability
   A.5 Specification-implementation alignment
   A.6 Requirement conflict identification
   A.7 Implicit requirement surfacing
   A.8 Requirement testability assessment
   A.9 Stakeholder requirement coverage
   A.10 Regulatory/legal requirement compliance

B. USER-CENTERED DIMENSIONS
   B.1 User usecase coverage and completeness
   B.2 User story quality (clear, testable, valuable)
   B.3 User flow analysis (happy path + edge cases)
   B.4 Usability heuristic violation detection
   B.5 User intent alignment (stated vs. actual)
   B.6 Accessibility gap identification
   B.7 User error tolerance and recovery
   B.8 User cognitive load assessment
   B.9 User emotional response prediction
   B.10 User trust and confidence factors

C. SYSTEM & TECHNICAL DIMENSIONS
   C.1 System flow coherence
   C.2 Architecture fitness for purpose
   C.3 System design consistency
   C.4 Individual function design quality
   C.5 Feature completeness vs. over-engineering
   C.6 Performance characteristic adequacy
   C.7 Resource usage efficiency
   C.8 Data structure appropriateness
   C.9 Error handling completeness
   C.10 Failure mode analysis
   C.11 Scalability assessment
   C.12 Maintainability evaluation
   C.13 Integration point robustness
   C.14 Data flow integrity
   C.15 Security posture (where applicable)

D. MARKET & BUSINESS DIMENSIONS
   D.1 Target market validation
   D.2 Ideal Customer Profile (ICP) accuracy
   D.3 Competitive positioning strength
   D.4 Market timing assessment
   D.5 Pricing model fit
   D.6 Distribution channel adequacy
   D.7 Customer acquisition cost realism
   D.8 Lifetime value assumptions
   D.9 Market size estimation validity
   D.10 Go-to-market strategy coherence

E. HUMAN PROCESS DIMENSIONS (Core AHPAS Focus)
   E.1 Individual judgment quality and consistency
   E.2 Verbal and written communication patterns
   E.3 Relationship dynamics and effects on outcomes
   E.4 Tribal knowledge inventory and risk
   E.5 Hierarchy effects on error reporting/correction
   E.6 Fatigue and stress impacts on decisions
   E.7 Cross-shift/cross-team knowledge transfer
   E.8 Team composition and stability effects
   E.9 Decision-making process quality
   E.10 Learning culture effectiveness
   E.11 Knowledge documentation vs. tribal knowledge
   E.12 Communication frequency optimization
   E.13 Meeting effectiveness
   E.14 Feedback loop presence and quality
   E.15 Succession planning adequacy

F. PLANNING DIMENSIONS (Planning Mode)
   F.1 Assumption realism (vs. historical similar cases)
   F.2 Plan completeness (vs. failure taxonomy)
   F.3 Team prospective fit analysis
   F.4 Blind spot detection (vs. historical patterns)
   F.5 Assumption conflict identification
   F.6 Prediction calibration assessment
   F.7 Risk coverage adequacy
   F.8 Resource allocation realism
   F.9 Timeline feasibility
   F.10 Financial projection validity
```

## Section 8 (Expanded): Complete Guardrails and Limitations

### 8.1 System Guardrails

```
G1. BIAS LAUNDERING PREVENTION
    Risk: Sycophantic LLM writes soft questions → Jev judges soft 
          questions → analysis isn't adversarial
    Mitigation:
    - Generate battery under multiple hostile personas
    - Human-review pinned battery once (amortized cost)
    - Jev meta-judgment: "Is this question actually adversarial?"

G2. CIRCULAR REASONING PREVENTION
    Risk: Same LLM generates questions AND interprets results → 
          errors compound
    Mitigation:
    - Split roles: different prompts (or models) for authoring vs. synthesis
    - Jev's judgments are ground truth synthesizer must respect
    - "Do not invent findings not present in judgments" (hard constraint)

G3. CANDIDATE COVERAGE ENFORCEMENT
    Risk: LLM omits real failure mode → Jev can't find it
    Mitigation:
    - Always include "other/none of these" branch
    - When "other" wins: loop back for candidate regeneration
    - Human expert validates option completeness

G4. COST INVERSION AVOIDANCE
    Risk: Re-running LLM generation destroys Jev's cost advantage
    Mitigation:
    - Compile once, pin, re-judge
    - Only regenerate on major artifact changes
    - Track cost per analysis cycle

G5. CONFIDENCE MISREADING PREVENTION
    Risk: Treating low Choice confidence as "wrong answer"
    Mitigation:
    - Per docs: several acceptable alternatives can spread probability
    - Low confidence on harmless preference is fine
    - Escalate on unused-branch uncertainty ignored logic
    - Train users on correct confidence interpretation

G6. TRUTH GUARANTEE CLARIFICATION
    Risk: Assuming typed output = truth
    Mitigation:
    - "Typed output guarantees the interface, not truth" (from docs)
    - System One models trained for calibrated decisions
    - Validate performance in target domain
    - Human expert review for high-stakes findings
```

### 8.2 Planning Mode Limitations

```
L1. CANNOT PREDICT BLACK SWANS
    Plans fail for reasons no similar case anticipated.
    AHPAS finds predictable failure modes, not unprecedented ones.
    Historical reference: Companies that succeeded despite "bad" plans 
    because market conditions changed unpredictably.

L2. CANNOT REPLACE CONVICTION
    The most successful ventures often looked "bad" on paper.
    Pattern-matching against history would have rejected them.
    Examples: Airbnb (rejected by 7 VCs using pattern matching), 
    Tesla (multiple near-death experiences that pattern analysis 
    would have flagged as fatal).
    AHPAS surfaces risks; humans decide which risks to accept.

L3. CANNOT JUDGE NOVEL INNOVATION
    Truly unprecedented plans have no similar cases.
    System degrades gracefully:
    - Analyzes internal consistency
    - Evaluates team quality
    - Checks assumption coherence
    - But cannot benchmark against history

L4. CANNOT CAPTURE LUCK
    Success/failure includes massive variance.
    Similar plans with identical quality succeed and fail.
    System gives probabilities, not certainties.
    Reference class survivorship bias is unavoidable.

L5. THE REFERENCE CLASS PROBLEM
    "Similar" is always approximate.
    Choosing reference class is itself a judgment that can bias 
    everything downstream.
    Example: Is a 2024 AI startup similar to 2015 SaaS companies?
    The answer determines all subsequent analysis.

L6. TEMPORAL DRIFT
    Historical patterns may not apply to changed conditions.
    Market cycles, technology shifts, regulatory changes can 
    invalidate historical patterns.
    System includes recency weighting but cannot fully solve this.

L7. SELF-FULFILLING PROPHECY RISK
    If everyone uses similar analysis, plans converge toward 
    "safe" patterns, reducing diversity of approaches.
    Innovation requires variance that pattern-matching suppresses.

L8. HUMAN PROCESS DATA AVAILABILITY
    Without Layer 2 data (human process data), analysis quality 
    degrades significantly.
    Organizations must often create this data through retrospective 
    reconstruction or prospective instrumentation.
    This is expensive and time-consuming but essential.

L9. DOMAIN EXPERT DEPENDENCE
    System requires domain expert validation for:
    - Question battery review
    - Finding interpretation
    - Recommendation cultural fit
    - Similar-case selection validation
    Without expert involvement, false positives are likely.
```

---

## Planning Mode Additions

### Complete Planning Mode Escalation Cascade

```
PLANNING MODE ESCALATION:

LEVEL 1: JEV TRIAGE ON PLAN
├── Run all planning judgments (assumption realism, completeness, 
│   team fit, etc.)
├── Flag: assumptions with realism probability < 0.3
├── Flag: completeness gaps with impact > 0.7
├── Flag: team composition risks with confidence > 0.6
├── Flag: blind spot patterns matching historical failures
└── Flag: prediction calibration issues (overoptimism factor > 2x)

LEVEL 2: PI-AUTORESEARCH INVESTIGATION
├── For flagged assumptions:
│   ├── Retrieve additional similar cases
│   ├── Refine reference class selection
│   └── Quantify impact of assumption being wrong
├── For completeness gaps:
│   ├── Analyze failure taxonomy coverage
│   ├── Identify specific missing elements
│   └── Historical impact of similar gaps
├── For team risks:
│   ├── Analyze team composition patterns
│   ├── Identify specific role gaps
│   └── Historical success with similar modifications
└── For calibration issues:
    ├── Analyze creator's full prediction history
    ├── Apply calibration factor to all projections
    └── Generate adjusted projections

LEVEL 3: LLM DEEP CONTEXTUAL ANALYSIS
├── Read full planning artifacts for nuance
├── Identify unstated assumptions (from what's NOT said)
├── Surface narrative inconsistencies
├── Analyze revision history (optimism cascade detection)
├── Draft specific modifications with evidence
└── Generate counterfactual scenarios

LEVEL 4: HUMAN EXPERT REVIEW
├── Domain expert validates similar-case selection
├── Entrepreneur/creator provides context on flagged risks
├── Decide which risks to accept vs. mitigate
├── Approve modified plan or consciously accept risks
└── Document decision rationale for future learning
```

### Gap 1.3: Critical Guardrails Table

**Status: ✅ FULLY INTEGRATED**

**Location:** Section 8.1 "System Guardrails" (G1-G6)

**Evidence of all 6 guardrails:**

```
G1. BIAS LAUNDERING PREVENTION
    Risk: Sycophantic LLM writes soft questions
    Mitigation: Multiple hostile personas, human-review, Jev meta-judgment

G2. CIRCULAR REASONING PREVENTION  
    Risk: Same LLM generates questions AND interprets results
    Mitigation: Split roles, Jev as ground truth, hard constraint

G3. CANDIDATE COVERAGE ENFORCEMENT
    Risk: LLM omits real failure mode
    Mitigation: "Other/none" branch, regeneration loop, expert validation

G4. COST INVERSION AVOIDANCE
    Risk: Re-running LLM generation
    Mitigation: Compile once, pin, re-judge; regenerate only on major changes

G5. CONFIDENCE MISREADING PREVENTION
    Risk: Treating low Choice confidence as wrong answer
    Mitigation: Correct interpretation training, escalation logic

G6. TRUTH GUARANTEE CLARIFICATION
    Risk: Assuming typed output = truth
    Mitigation: "Interface, not truth" disclaimer, domain validation, 
    expert review
```

**Planning Mode Integration:** All 6 guardrails apply; Planning Mode spec Section "System Guardrails" references Section 8.1.

Cost Modeling by Project Size:
- Small (100 functions): LLM $5 vs. Hybrid $1

```
TIER 1: Code Validation

- Financial model internal consistency

TIER 2: Jev Meta-Judgment on Plan Questions
- "Does this planning question conflate multiple assumptions?"
- "Are success criteria for this plan measurable?"
- "Does this reference class selection bias the analysis?"
- Failed questions → regeneration

TIER 3: Human Expert Review
- Domain expert validates similar-case selection
- Checks for survivorship bias in reference class
- Validates adversarial question appropriateness
- Approves battery for planning analysis
```

### Gap 2.2: Escalation Cascade for Planning Mode

**Status: ✅ FULLY INTEGRATED**

**Location:** Planning Mode Section "Complete Planning Mode Escalation Cascade"

**Evidence:** All 4 levels present:

```
LEVEL 1: Jev Triage on Plan
- Flag assumptions with realism probability < 0.3
- Flag completeness gaps with impact > 0.7
- Flag team composition risks with confidence > 0.6
- Flag blind spot patterns matching historical failures
- Flag prediction calibration issues (overoptimism > 2x)

LEVEL 2: pi-autoresearch Investigation
- Retrieve additional similar cases
- Refine reference class selection
- Quantify impact of assumption being wrong
- Analyze failure taxonomy coverage
- Apply calibration factor to all projections

LEVEL 3: LLM Deep Contextual Analysis
- Read full planning artifacts for nuance
- Identify unstated assumptions
- Surface narrative inconsistencies
- Analyze revision history (optimism cascade)
- Draft specific modifications with evidence

LEVEL 4: Human Expert Review
- Domain expert validates similar-case selection
- Entrepreneur/creator provides context
- Decide which risks to accept vs. mitigate
- Document decision rationale
```

### Gap 3.2: Complete Question Battery Templates

**Status: ✅ FULLY INTEGRATED**

**Location:** Section "Complete Question Battery Template Library"

**Evidence of all template categories:**

```
DECISION QUALITY BATTERIES:
- Choice: "What primarily drove this decision?" (5 options)
- Noul: "Was this decision adequately justified?"
- Score: "How well did this decision balance concerns?" (5 criteria)

COMMUNICATION EFFECTIVENESS:
- Noul: "Did this communication transfer needed information?"
- Score: "How well was context preserved?" (5 criteria)
- Choice: "What information category was lost?" (5 options)

TEAM DYNAMICS:
- Noul: "Did team familiarity drive this outcome?"
- Score: "How effectively could team members voice concerns?" (5 criteria)
- Choice: "What team composition gap existed?" (5 options)

KNOWLEDGE TRANSFER:
- Noul: "Would knowledge transfer survive departure?"
- Choice: "What knowledge exists only in this person's head?" (5 options)
- Score: "How well is tribal knowledge documented?" (5 criteria)

PLANNING-SPECIFIC:
- Noul: "Is this assumption realistic given history?"
- Score: "How well calibrated is creator's predictions?" (5 criteria)
- Choice: "Which failure category does plan NOT address?" (7 options)
- Noul: "Do these assumptions conflict?"
```
